Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I, um, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this. It's probably nothing too terrible, really, but... well, I guess I just don’t have the push I used to have. 

I used to always be doing something, you know? Choir, my quilting, or just going out and visiting folks. But lately, I just... I mostly just sit at home all day with the TV on real low. I want to be good about it and do what I'm supposed to, I really do.

I want to be good about it and do what I'm supposed to, I really do. \n\nIs... is that an okay reason to come see you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by being here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself, because I'm really not. I have a lot to be thankful for, I truly do. \n\nIt's not sadness, and I don't feel... empty, exactly. I just feel kind of... well, just still, I suppose. I'm not unhappy, I just can't seem to muster up the energy to do my usual things. I hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I don't enjoy them anymore. I mean, I love my quilting group, and singing in the choir is always such a blessing. I wouldn't want anyone to think I don't appreciate those things, because I really do. \n\nIt's just... when I'm actually doing them, I suppose I don't get that same little spark I used to get. It's not that I dislike it, it's just... a bit flat, maybe? But I still think they're wonderful activities. I hope that doesn't sound ungrateful. Is it alright if I feel that way sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're looking forward to things less often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... maybe a little bit less? But I don't want you to think I'm a pessimist or anything like that. I still look forward to seeing my grandchildren, of course, and I always hope for good weather. \n\nI guess I just don't have that same... excitement bubbling up for the everyday things like I used to. But I know everyone slows down a little at my age, right? That's just normal, isn't it? I just want to be sure I'm not making a fuss over something that's perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I've lost interest. I still care very much about my little projects and my family. I don't want you to think I've just given up or that I'm sitting around being lazy, because I certainly haven't. \n\nI still want to be involved in things, truly I do. I try my very best to keep busy. I think it's so important to stay active and be a good member of the community, don't you? I'm trying to do exactly what I'm supposed to do, just keeping on with things as best I can. I hope I'm being helpful to you today with these answers... am I doing alright, Doctor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know people are expecting me at these things, and I feel just awful about letting them down, but I just can't seem to get going. I want to be good about it and do what I'm supposed to, I really do. \n\nIs... is that an okay reason to come see you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by being here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself, because I'm really not. I have a lot to be thankful for, I truly do. \n\nIt's not sadness, and I don't feel... empty, exactly. I just feel kind of... well, just still, I suppose. I'm not unhappy, I just can't seem to muster up the energy to do my usual things. I hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I don't enjoy them anymore. I mean, I love my quilting group, and singing in the choir is always such a blessing. I wouldn't want anyone to think I don't appreciate those things, because I really do. \n\nIt's just... when I'm actually doing them, I suppose I don't get that same little spark I used to get. It's not that I dislike it, it's just... a bit flat, maybe? But I still think they're wonderful activities. I hope that doesn't sound ungrateful. I just want to be sure I'm not making a fuss over something that's perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I've lost interest. Well, I... I suppose I might have to push just a little bit more than I used to. But that's just getting older, isn't it? It's perfectly normal to need a minute to get going.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I wouldn't say I can't feel happy at all. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself or being miserable. I always try to count my blessings. 

But... I suppose it goes back to what I was saying before. The things that always brought me so much joy, like the choir or my quilting, or going out visiting folks... I just don't have the push for them right now. Even when I think about how much I used to love it, that... that warm, happy feeling is just a little hard to find. 

So I just end up sitting at home with the TV on low, even though I know the ladies are expecting me to be there, smiling and joining in. I feel just terrible that I'm not getting the same joy out of it right now. I'm trying to look on the bright side, though, I really am. I hope I'm explaining this right for you, Doctor. Does that make sense?

I wouldn't say I'm tired all the time. I make sure to get up and do my chores every morning. I don't want to be one of those people who just sleeps the day away, you know? I try very hard to keep my energy up. \n\nI mean, maybe by the evening I'm a little worn out, but... well, at sixty-seven, needing a little rest after supper is perfectly acceptable, isn't it? I'm certainly not trying to complain about my energy or anything. I just try to do my fair share around the house. I hope that's alright to say, Doctor. I'm doing okay with that, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, no, I... I eat just fine, really I do. I always make sure to finish what's on my plate. I know how important it is to keep your strength up, and I certainly don't want to be fussy about my meals or waste good food. \n\nI try to eat my regular portions every day, just exactly like you're supposed to. I haven't noticed it going up or down or anything like that. I hope that's a good sign... it's good that I'm still eating well, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about not doing things you used to do?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, heavens, no. I don't feel guilty. I... I do what I can, you know? I still keep up with the things that matter most, and I certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself or dwelling on things I can't do anymore. \n\nI mean, everyone slows down a little bit at my age, right? It's just natural. I try to focus on being useful where I am needed now, instead of worrying about the past. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting here feeling bad about myself, because I'm not. I'm keeping a very positive attitude about it all. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting as much pleasure out of the things you're still doing as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, pleasure? Yes, absolutely. I... I still enjoy my days very much. I make sure to appreciate the little things, you know? Just... keeping things tidy or having a nice cup of tea in the afternoon. \n\nI certainly don't sit around moping or finding fault with everything. I think it's so important to stay cheerful and be grateful for what you have, rather than complaining. I'm doing my best to keep a smile on my face, Doctor. I'm doing the right thing by staying upbeat, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how quickly or slowly you move compared to how you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, my moving? No, I... Well, I... I suppose it hasn't been a sudden thing. I wouldn't want you to think I just woke up one day and decided to stop trying. It's been more of a... a gradual slowing down, I guess you could say.

I know people are expecting me at these things, and I feel just awful about letting them down, but I just can't seem to get going. I want to be good about it and do what I'm supposed to, I really do. \n\nIs... is that an okay reason to come see you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by being here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself, because I'm really not. I have a lot to be thankful for, I truly do. \n\nIt's not sadness, and I don't feel... empty, exactly. I just feel kind of... well, just still, I suppose. I'm not unhappy, I just can't seem to muster up the energy to do my usual things. I hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I don't enjoy them anymore. I mean, I love my quilting group, and singing in the choir is always such a blessing. I wouldn't want anyone to think I don't appreciate those things, because I really do. \n\nIt's just... when I'm actually doing them, I suppose I don't get that same little spark I used to get. It's not that I dislike it, it's just... a bit flat, maybe? But I still think they're wonderful activities. I hope that doesn't sound ungrateful. I'm doing okay with that, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, my appetite? Well, no, I... I eat just fine, really I do. I always make sure to finish what's on my plate. I know how important it is to keep your strength up, and I certainly don't want to be fussy about my meals or waste good food. \n\nI try to eat my regular portions every day, just exactly like you're supposed to. I haven't noticed it going up or down or anything like that. I hope that's a good sign... it's good that I'm still eating well, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about not doing things you used to do?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, heavens, no. I don't feel guilty. I... I do what I can, you know? I still keep up with the things that matter most, and I certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself or dwelling on things I can't do anymore. \n\nI mean, everyone slows down a little bit at my age, right? It's just natural. I try to focus on being useful where I am needed now, instead of worrying about the past. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting here feeling bad about myself, because I'm not. I'm keeping a very positive attitude about it all. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting as much pleasure out of the things you're still doing as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, pleasure? Yes, absolutely. I... I still enjoy my days very much. I make sure to appreciate the little things, you know? Just... keeping things tidy or having a nice cup of tea in the afternoon. \n\nI certainly don't sit around moping or finding fault with everything. I think it's so important to stay cheerful and be grateful for what you have, rather than complaining. I'm doing my best to keep a smile on my face, Doctor. I'm doing the right thing by staying upbeat, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how quickly or slowly you move compared to how you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, my moving? No, I... I keep up with everything just exactly like I always have. I'm doing my absolute best to stay active and not be a burden to anybody... staying on your feet and keeping busy is the best way to be, isn't it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that used to give you pleasure, now feel less enjoyable to you?\n\nPatient: Less enjoyable? I'm doing a good job of keeping my spirits up, aren't I, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor. I... well, like I was saying just a minute ago, I really do still enjoy things just fine. I promise I haven't lost my ability to smile. I still like doing the things I always did, honest. \n\nI'm not giving up or letting myself go... I swear it. I try very hard to be a happy person and appreciate the good Lord's blessings. You don't have to worry about me being ungrateful or anything like that. I'm doing okay, aren't I? I just hope I'm answering all these questions right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to push yourself to do things that you used to do without thinking?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I'm sure I'll bounce back soon.\n\nDoctor: How many months would you say you've been feeling like you don't have the push to do things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, I'm sorry, Doctor, I should have figured that out for you a moment ago. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this lack of push has been going on for less than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, I must have completely confused you, Doctor.
